美債市場結構性轉變：從不挑剔的機構到追求回報的私人投資者
• 自新冠疫情以來 ，美國10年期公債殖利率持續走高 ，過去市場多半歸咎於聯準會升息或通膨 ，但彭博最新研究顯示 ，期限
溢價的顯著上升才是主要推手，其背後的根本原因是美債買家發生了結構性轉變。
• 傳統非價格敏感型買家縮手：過去幾十年，聯準會(透過QE)與外國央行是美債的兩大底氣來源，他們買美債是為了儲備與流
動性，對價格不敏感，因此能有效壓低利率與期限溢價 。自2020年以來，聯準會與外國官方持有的美債佔美國 GDP比重分
別下降12%與8%，光是這兩大買家的退場，就推升10年期美債期限溢價約1.05%。
• 私人投資者接棒，市場對殖利率極度挑剔： 目前的邊際買家已轉為外國私人投資者 (如基金、保險公司等)，私人買家會精算

美債、公司債、股票與各國主權債的收益差異，進行嚴格比價。當美債發行量增加時，必須給予更高的殖利率才能吸引他們
購買，這使得美債利率波動更加劇烈。
自疫情以來，長期利率呈上升趨勢
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
主要機構買家持有的美國國債減少

顧
供給端新挑戰：AI浪潮帶動公司債暴增，搶奪資金池
• 科技巨頭發債融資潮 ，公司債成為美債的強勁替代品： 受人工智慧建設資本支出帶動 ，大型科技企業 (如Alphabet、Meta、
Amazon等)積極發行投資級公司債 。這些高品質科技公司債提供優異回報 ，吸引了大量私人資金從美債轉向公司債 。為了
與這些高收益的高評級公司債競爭資金，美國國債殖利率面臨更大的上行壓力。
• 小結及建議：當前美債殖利率居高不下，核心原因在於穩定的買家減少，而接手的私人買家變得挑剔，加上AI熱潮創造了高
回報的公司債競爭源。在美國財政部發債壓力未減的情況下，長端殖利率短期內難大幅下行。債券策略建議採取配置中短天
期債券，長天期公債(10年期以上)價格受期限溢價與供給壓力壓制，波動較大，建議縮短持債年期(如布局3-5年期)，鎖定高

殖利率的同時降低價格波動風險 ，加碼高品質投資級公司債，目前私人資金偏好高評級公司債(尤其是科技與AI龍頭債)，這
類債券信用風險極低，收益率卻優於同天期公債，是當前固定收益資產的優質選擇。
外國私人投資者購買美國公債和公司債
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
人工智慧相關債務發行只是殖利率上升的原因之一

日圓再現匯率干預跡象 套利交易平倉恐加劇日圓升值
• 日圓再現匯率干預跡象：上週週間，日圓兌美元匯價出現單日大幅升值情況，市場再度懷疑官方出手干預匯率。然而，彭博
指出，日本央行帳戶數據沒有大規模買入日元跡象，意味著匯率的劇烈波動可能來自市場重新調整日本央行加息預期，而非
日本政府再次入市干預。與此同時，美國財政部並未回應是否參與干預。
• 市場懷疑干預之際，套利交易平倉可能正在發生：儘管無法證實官方上週是否直接干預日圓匯價，但日圓走升可能也反映上
週日本央行官員高田創釋出鷹派升息的態度，將不排除一次性大幅升息或連續升息的可能性。根據芝加哥商品交易所的數據
顯示，日圓融資的套利交易正在平倉，而且中期內持有日圓而非其他G10貨幣的意願非常強烈。
• 觀點：2024年8月日本央行意外升息，引發日圓套利交易平倉，全球主要國家股市短期間出現修正，當時AI與半導體成為套

利資金撤離重點。本次匯價波動暫時可歸咎於套利交易倉位的調整，尚未出現恐慌賣壓，重點將關注在本月中的日本央行利
率會議，留意意外大幅升息的風險。

• 今年年初黃金價格創下歷史新高，突破每盎司5,000美元大關，成為金融市場最受關注的資產之一，隨後經歷幾個月的高檔
修正後，上個月再度獲得市場資金追捧，現貨黃金價格單月大漲近10%。過去市場認為黃金上漲主要來自地緣政治風險與央
行降息預期，但近期投資邏輯似乎正在改變。
• 黃金多頭始自央行「去美元化」：本輪黃金多頭始自2022年烏俄衝突，西方國家凍結俄羅斯央行的大部分海外美元與歐元
外匯儲備，讓各國央行開始意識到外匯儲備的安全性，進而推動外匯儲備多元化。透過適度減持美元(美債)來佈局實體黃金
或其他國家貨幣，被成為「去美元化」趨勢。
• 「貶值交易」有望接棒黃金上漲動能：上個月美國公布最新國債規模已突破40兆美元，市場開始擔憂財政赤字的可維持性，
貨幣信心下降導致「貶值交易」發生(美債信心下降)，除了導致長天期美債殖利率上揚之外，貨幣避險需求再度讓黃金資產

成為市場焦點，8月份現貨黃金一度上漲超過15%，隨後雖因聯準會釋出鷹派訊號，單月漲幅仍接近10%。
貶值交易趨勢下，黃金的投資機會
全球央行黃金儲備佔比
資料來源：世界黃金協會、富蘭克林投顧，截至2025年，兆豐銀行整理。

• 央行買盤仍為黃金中期表現帶來利多：根據世界黃金協會2026年各國央行調查，上半年截至5/19，有45%的央行預期未來
一年將增加自身黃金儲備，創調查9年來新高。央行增持黃金主要考量包含：儲備多元化、美元走弱預期、地緣政治衝突以
及金價長期上漲趨勢，顯示黃金已成為各國儲備資產的重要戰略配置，在強勁的需求買盤下，多數機構預測黃金將維持多頭
至明年年底。
• 觀點：近期黃金價格反彈後，可能會受到獲利了結以及利率政策預期變化而出現波動，但在全球債務問題、貨幣貶值風險及
央行買盤支撐下，在中期內仍有機會走出慢牛行情。建議投資人以資產配置的角度，逢修正適度配置黃金資產(如：黃金ETF)。
風險接受度較高的投資人，則可關注金礦類股，但需注意其對於美元匯、利率的敏感度較高，資產價格波動較大。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

Telecom & Networking Equipment  | North America
Potential FCC Rules on Optical
Transceivers More Likely to
Come in at 3.2T
Recent DC meetings highlight 3.2T as the most likely generation
to see restrictions on China-based optical transceivers. This
echoes conversations with LITE during the Q noting that any
restrictions would need to be phased in. See potential rules
keeping laser market tight, a pos for LITE/COHR.
Morgan Stanley & Co. LLC

Morgan Stanley MUFG Securities Co., Ltd.+
Yu Shirakawa
Equity Analyst
Yu.Shirakawa@morganstanleymufg.com                                                +81 3 6836-5432
Telecom & Networking Equipment
North America
Industry View In-Line
What Happened: Morgan Stanley strategists recently  met with a senior Washington
telecom, FCC and export-control legal team with direct engagement across the
White House and FCC. Discussion focused on the Administration’s efforts to reduce

reliance on Chinese technology, with the FCC Covered List emerging as the more
likely near-term mechanism for optical transceivers (a note we made in our AI Safety
DC trip wrap-up last week). Policy discussions suggest 3.2T transceivers would likely
be where restrictions would be phased in, potentially as soon as October, with
industry recently asked to demonstrate credible U.S. or friend-shoring plans before
3.2T becomes broadly deployed. Our conversations noted that discussions are also

indicating Chinese-built transceivers could still be importable, even at 3.2T, as long
as 65% of the BOM comes from US-based companies (DSP / laser would get you
pretty close). Initial 3.2T volumes are not expected to ramp in volume until 2029,
limiting the near-term revenue impact of a restriction even if action comes sooner.
We would see these rules as having the potential to avoid creating a constraint
on hyperscalers' ability to build today, but keeping the  laser market tighter given

laser would largely need to be from a US-based vendor to meet a 65% value
threshold, a positive for LITE/COHR.
How this echoes conversation with LITE earlier in the quarter: We hosted LITE at
Nasdaq Asia in late August, where we asked the company about potential FCC rules.
At the time they noted that the FCC had been in contact with them about potential
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Recent DC trip by our strategist   team gave a greater idea of what potential FCC
rules on optical transceivers could look like.
Potential restrictions, likely to come in at 3.2T, limit Chinese-based transceivers,
with a potential workaround if US-based content above 65% of the BOM.
Solution seemingly a win-win for LITE/COHR, with laser market likely to stay

tight, but not having to take on lower module assembly business.
Also, does not create bottlenecks for hyperscalers.
Assuring InP substrate delivery will be key to look at going forward.
October 1, 2026 04:09 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
Update
2
rules, with the company noting their feedback was that the rules would need to be
phased in over time to prevent material supply/demand dislocation in the market
with 60-65% of transceivers coming from China-based transceiver vendors. They
also noted that pure transceiver volumes alone, at current pricing structures, would
not be the most attractive business for them to bring on. These rules, which are
both phased in and don't limit Chinese assemblers, as long as enough content is

US-based, would seemingly address both of these concerns.
Our Thoughts:
1. 3.2T looks like the likely policy pressure point, but the near-term impact
should be limited - Administration efforts appear focused on reshaping the
supply chain before 3.2T reaches volume rather than disrupting the existing
800G and 1.6T base. With the 3.2T ramp not expected to begin until 2028
and broader adoption closer to 2029, an October Covered List action would
provide roughly two years before meaningful volumes enter the market.

Hyperscalers are already working on plans intended to demonstrate greater
U.S. or friend-shored manufacturing, suggesting the policy objective is
increasingly about determining where the next generation is built rather
than immediately displacing current Chinese supply.
2. Laser pricing looks like the stronger read-through versus transceiver
share - A Covered List framework does not necessarily require Chinese
module vendors to lose significant share if they can increase the U.S.

content of the underlying BOM. Combining a U.S. DSP from Marvell with
laser content from LITE/COHR could potentially move a Chinese-built
transceiver to the 65% U.S.-value threshold discussed in our strategists
team policy meeting. Such an outcome would leave Innolight and Eoptolink
(both covered by Andy Meng) with less flexibility to substitute toward
China-based lasers while allowing them to remain relevant at the module
level, reducing the risk from China-based laser makers. We therefore see a

stronger read-through for high-speed laser pricing and LITE/COHR
component share than for a wholesale redistribution of transceiver share.
3. Replacement capacity not ready; nor is it the most attractive opportunity
- As noted above, LITE noted that assembly of transceivers (mid-30's to low-
40s gross margin product), is not the most attractive opportunity for US-
based optical companies (vs. EMLs and lasers well north of 50% steady
state and approaching 75% today). While 3.2T is not yet in production, we

use AAOI's current 800G and 1.6T capacity ramp as an illustration of the
scale of US supply available to absorb a potential future demand shift. AAOI
(not covered), the only material US-based production of transceivers, is
currently ramping capacity. AAOI exited Q2 with manufacturing capacity
approaching 200,000 800G and 1.6T units per month and expects to reach
~650,000 units per month by year-end 2026, while management continues
to expect demand to outpace production through mid-2027. AAOI’s YE26

capacity annualizes to ~12M 800G/1.6T units, versus ~101M units
combined for Innolight and Eoptolink based on annualized 1H capacity
(44.3M and 56.7M, respectively), or >8x AAOI. Based on AAOI’s year-end
capacity plans and our Internet team’s hyperscaler capex work, we estimate
AAOI could support less than 4 GW of hyperscaler capacity even before

### Page 3

M
Update
Morgan Stanley Research 3
factoring in manufacturing utilization. Replacing a meaningful portion of
Innolight and Eoptolink’s supply would therefore require a substantial multi-
year capacity build, with limited ability for existing U.S. suppliers to absorb
those volumes in the near term.
4. InP supply and sourcing outside of China will still be areas to watch -
Potential FCC rules could create a win-win for LITE/COHR by reducing the
risk of China-based laser makers flooding the market, without forcing them

to build significant transceiver module capacity to absorb lower-margin
assembly business. Were China to view the rules as too one-sided, it could
respond with tighter restrictions on InP substrate exports, a key bottleneck
to laser capacity ramping. At the same time, sourcing outside of China could
broaden the beneficiary set beyond U.S. suppliers, supporting InP substrate
demand at JX Advanced Metals (5016 JP), while providing a tailwind for
Sumitomo Electric’s (5802 JP) CWLD products and Furukawa Electric’s

(5801 JP) DFB lasers.

## Document 085: 261006_daiwa_kinsus

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_daiwa_kinsus.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We believe an improving product mix, driven by rising 
Nvidia’s CPU contribution, to be a key near-term share price catalyst.

What’s the impact: Near-term update. After Kinsus released its August 
preliminary results on 23 September, we note that we had underestimated 
the 3Q26E gross margin expansion on product optimisation and price hike 
scale. The results imply a GM of 33% in August (vs. 26% in 2Q26). We 
therefore lift our 3Q-4Q26E gross margin to 33.2-35.1% (from 28.9-30.3%) 
to reflect an improving product mix, including greater CPU substrate 
contribution. We believe the gross margin of high-end chipset substrate 
may be c.50%, supporting margin expansion. We also expect a c.10% QoQ 
price hike for both BT and ABF substrate in 3Q26, above Kinsus’ guidance 
of a high-single-digit% QoQ, making these substrates the other growth 
driver. We see further gross margin expansion in 2027-28 on the ongoing 
price hike trend and increasing high-end ABF substrate product mix.

Business outlook. We believe several favourable trends lie ahead. First, 
we see a rising contribution from high-end GPU and CPU ABF substrates. 
We believe Nvidia’s CPU currently accounts for 5-10% of total revenue, 
with Kinsus holding a 70-80% market share. We also expect Kinsus to 
acquire a teens% market share in AMD’s Venice, which should gradually 
ramp up from 4Q26. These will likely become key growth drivers of the ABF 
substrate segment in 2027, in our view. We believe the ramp-up progress 
for GPU substrate for these two clients is slightly slower than that for CPU. 
Second, we believe Kinsus will benefit from the rising BT substrate demand 
for optical transceivers. In our view, migration to on-board optics (OBO) and 
NPO/CPO will require ABF substrate beneath the optics engine (OE) in 
OBO; the OE and IC will need a bigger ABF substrate as we move to the 
NPO/CPO generation. As such, we expect the total ABF substrate demand 
to increase with rising adoption of optics in AI servers. Lastly, we expect the 
adoption of multiple-core ABF from 2028 to improve the rigidity and 
transmission performance before the official launch of ceramic and glass-
core substrate, which we expect to increase the ABF TAM as well.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 19-29% to 
reflect the better-than-expected gross margin in August. We reaffirm our 
Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP to TWD1,580, based on an unchanged 
target PER of 36x (high end of 3-year-trading range of 10-36x), applied to 
the 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risks: weaker-than-
expected demand for memory, PCs, gaming GPUs or AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 27-57% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish view on its gross margin expansion.

6 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: several favourable trends ahead

➢ Rising high-end ABF substrate likely to drive blended ASP increase   
➢ Multicore and optics likely to be the next growth drivers 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD1,580

Company profile  
Kinsus was founded in 2000 as a subsidiary of Asustek. The company mainly focus on niche ball grid 
array (BGA) substrates to avoid price competition among mainstream products like graphics card and 
chipsets, and it has become top-3 flip chip manufacturer in global. Besides, its manufacturing sites are 
based in Taiwan and mainland China.

### Page 5

5

Kinsus Interconnect Technology (3189 TT): 6 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 31 Jan 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Kinsus's leadership team comprises experienced professionals dedicated to steering the
company towards sustainable growth. This diverse board composition ensures a comprehensive
range of expertise and perspectives, contributing to Kinsus’s strategic decision-making and
corporate governamce.
Capital management 2
Kinsus maintains a robust financial framework, ensuring the availability of resources necessary
for operational efficiency and strategic investments. The company's financial health is regularly
disclosed through annual reports, providing transparency to stakeholders.
Related party &
transaction 1
The company adheres to stringent corporate governance policies to manage related party
transactions, ensuring transparency and compliance with regulatory standards. The company
discloses such transactions in its financial reports, maintaining accountability and stakeholder
trust. For instance, the company's annual general meeting documents provide details on related
party transactions, ensuring transparency and accountability.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Kinsus is committed to sustainable supply chain practices, emphasizing environmental, social,
and governance considerations. The company has established comprehensive ESG policies,
including commitments to labor standards, environmental health and safety, ethics, and conflict-
free minerals. These policies guide the company's interactions with suppliers and partners,
promoting sustainability throughout the supply chain.
E Data security 3
The company recognizes the importance of information security in its operations. As part of its
corporate governance framework, Kinsus is likely to implement standard data protection
practices to safeguard sensitive information and ensure compliance with relevant regulations.
Stakeholders are encouraged to consult the company's official communications or contact the
company directly for detailed information on data security policies.

## Document 116: 9月新增信贷社融双双超预期，政府债券发行提速，居民融资需求仍偏弱

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月新增信贷社融双双超预期，政府债券发行提速，居民融资需求仍偏弱.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

9 ⽉新增⼈⺠币贷款 1.08 万亿、社融 3.55 万亿，双双超预期。政府债券发⾏加快是主要⽀
撑；企业短期融资延续偏弱，财政相关配套融资改善拉动企业中⻓期贷款好转；居⺠短
贷、中⻓贷均延续少增，指向地产、消费等经济内⽣动能仍然偏弱。后续货币宽松还是⼤
⽅向，降准降息仍有可能，关注地产销售能否回暖。
摘要：
事件：2026 年 9 ⽉新增⼈⺠币贷款 1.08 万亿，前值 600 亿，预期 9880 亿，去年同期 1.29 万亿；新
增社融 3.55 万亿，前值 1.66 万亿，预期 3.13 万亿，去年同期 3.53 万亿；存量社融增速 7.1% ，前
值 7.2% ； M2 同⽐ 7.2% ，预期 7.5% ，前值 7.5% ； M1 同⽐ 3.2% ，预期 3.9% ，前值 4.1% 。
核⼼观点：9 ⽉新增信贷社融规模双双超预期，政府债券发⾏加快是主要⽀撑；企业短期融资延
续偏弱，财政相关配套融资改善拉动企业中⻓期贷款好转；居⺠短贷、中⻓贷均延续少增，指
向地产、消费等经济内⽣动能仍然偏弱。此外，虽然政府债券发⾏加快，但从 M2 增速回落、财

政存款同⽐少减来看，财政资⾦落地仍有待加快，政策性⾦融⼯具投放也未有明显提速。往后
看，继续提⽰：政策短期重⼼应会强化抓落实、 “ ⽤好⽤⾜ ” 存量政策，包括加快政府债券、重⼤
项⽬和贴息政策落地等，后续更多增量政策也应会 “ 及时谋划 ” 。具体到货币端， “ 加⼤逆周期调
节⼒度 ” 定调下，宽松还是⼤⽅向，降准降息仍有可能。短期重点关注 9.28 以来⼀揽⼦稳增⻓政
策落地效果、尤其是地产销售能否回暖。
1 、整体看， 9 ⽉新增信贷社融规模双双超预期，但仍低于季节性，结构上未有明显改善。
2 、往后看，继续提⽰：货币宽松还是⼤⽅向，降准降息仍有可能。据 Q3 货政例会强调 “ 加⼤逆
周期调节⼒度 ” 、删除了 “ 加⼤跨周期调节⼒度 ” ，指向当前经济承压背景下，货币政策更加注重
“ 逆周期调节 ” ， 9.29 央⾏已宣布调降 PSL 利率等⼀揽⼦货币政策，后续降准降息仍有可能，重点
关注基本⾯的演化情况。
3 、短期看，有三点关注： 1 ）美伊局势、油价⾛势； 2 ） 9.28 以来⼀揽⼦稳增⻓政策的落地效

果，尤其是地产销售能否回暖； 3 ）财政政策落地效果，资⾦端关注政府债券发⾏节奏、政策性
⾦融⼯具落地情况，项⽬端关注重⼤项⽬部署、 “ 六⽹建设 ” 、基建实物⼯作量等。
正⽂如下：
1 、新增信贷略好于预期，但仍低于季节性，结构也未有好转，经济内⽣动能仍然偏弱。居⺠短
贷、中⻓期贷款均延续少增，指向消费、地产等经济内⽣动能仍偏弱。企业短期融资延续偏
弱，中⻓期融资边际好转，主要应与财政加快落地带动相关配套融资改善有关。
> 总量看， 9 ⽉新增信贷 1.08 万亿，同⽐少增 2100 亿，略好于预期（预期 9880 亿），但明显低于
季节性（近三年同期均为 1.73 万亿）。其中，居⺠部⻔新增贷款规模为 0 ，同⽐少增 3890 亿；企
业部⻔新增贷款 1.29 万亿，同⽐多增 700 亿；⾮银⾦融机构新增贷款 -1485 亿，同⽐少减 863 亿。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

> 居⺠加杠杆意愿仍不⾜，短贷、中⻓期贷款均延续少增，指向消费、地产等经济内⽣动能仍偏
弱。居⺠短期贷款为 -900 亿，同⽐少增 2321 亿，指向居⺠消费意愿仍偏弱。居⺠中⻓期贷款由
负转正⾄ 874 亿，同⽐少增 1626 亿，与同期房地产销售表现⼀致（ 9 ⽉ 30 ⼤中城商品房销售⾯积
同⽐ -10.8% 、降幅较上⽉进⼀步⾛阔， 11 城⼆⼿房销售⾯积同⽐由正转负⾄ -3.1% ）。后续关注
地产新政落地后能否带动销售边际回暖，从⾼频来看， 10 ⽉地产销售⽬前暂未看到明显改善迹
象。
> 企业短期融资延续偏弱，中⻓期融资边际好转，主要应与财政加快落地带动相关配套融资改善
有关。短期融资⽅⾯，企业短期贷款增加 4600 亿、同⽐少增 2500 亿；票据融资为 -500 亿，同⽐
少减 3526 亿，主要与去年同期企业信贷表现较好、票据冲量需求下降有关。企业中⻓期贷款增
加 8900 亿、同⽐少增 200 亿，表现略弱于季节性（近三年同期均值为 1.04 万亿），可能与近期财
政发⼒加快后，相关配套融资改善有关，反映实体企业融资意愿的 BCI 投资前瞻指数仍然延续下

⾏。

### Page 3

2 、新增社融规模好于预期，但弱于季节性，政府债券同⽐多增是主要拉动，存量社融增速较上
⽉回落 0.1 个百分点⾄ 7.1% 。
> 总量看， 9 ⽉新增社融 3.55 万亿，同⽐多增 192 亿，好于市场预期（市场预期 3.13 万亿），但仍
弱于季节性（近三年同期均值为 3.81 万亿），存量社融增速较上⽉回落 0.1 个百分点⾄ 7.1% 。
> 结构看， 9 ⽉社融⼝径⼈⺠币贷款增加 1.27 万亿，同⽐少增 3342 亿，是社融的主要拖累项。政
府债券新增 1.53 万亿，同⽐多增 3438 亿，指向政府债券发⾏明显加快。企业债券新增 -425 亿，
同⽐少增 561 亿，与企业短期融资放缓表现⼀致。表外融资⽅⾯，表外三项增加 2477 亿，同⽐少
增 1102 亿，表外票据增加 2153 亿、同⽐少增 1081 亿，主要与去年基数偏⾼有关；委托贷款新增
802 亿，指向政策性⾦融⼯具落地，但进度仍慢。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
3 、 M1 同⽐回落，基数⾛⾼、实体资⾦活化有限是主要拖累； M2 同⽐回落，财政投放偏慢、实
体信⽤扩张放缓是主要拖累。
>9 ⽉ M1 同⽐ 3.2% 、较上⽉回落 0.9 个百分点，基数⾛⾼是主要拖累，实体资⾦活化也未能形成
有效⽀撑。 M2 同⽐ 7.2% ，较上⽉回落 0.3 个百分点，信⽤扩张偏弱、财政投放偏慢是主要拖
累。存款⽅⾯， 9 ⽉存款增加 1.77 万亿，同⽐少增 4400 亿，其中⾮银⾦融机构存款减少 1.91 万
亿、同⽐多减 8500 亿，也是连续第 5 个⽉同⽐少增，指向股市⾛弱背景下，居⺠存款搬家明显放
缓；财政存款⽅⾯，减少 4300 亿、同⽐少减 4100 亿，扣除政府债券后同⽐少减 622 亿，指向财
政投放仍偏慢。
⻛险提⽰：经济超预期下⾏，外部环境超预期，政策超预期。

## Document 144: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간LG에너지솔루션(373220) Long Trading 구간Long Trading 구간 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호

RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

## Document 157: AI模型遭遇消费降级：顶级模型使用占比从55%降至14%，用户转向性价比

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI模型遭遇消费降级：顶级模型使用占比从55%降至14%，用户转向性价比.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI ⼤模型竞争正从追求极致性能转向注重性价⽐。 OpenRouter 数据显⽰，前⼗款⾼性能模
型的使⽤占⽐从 2025 年⼀季度的 55% 降⾄ 2026 年三季度的 14% 。 Meta 此时推出 Muse
Wonder ，试图证明⾃⾝的前沿模型实⼒，但性能领先能否转化为实际⽤⼾需求，仍有待市
场检验。
摘要：
AI ⼤模型⾏业正⾯临⼀个反差：科技巨头仍在竞相推出性能更强的前沿模型，⽤⼾却开始转向更
便宜、⾜以满⾜实际需求的产品。 Meta 此时推出 Muse Wonder ，试图证明⾃⾝的前沿模型实
⼒，但市场需求正从追求极致性能转向注重性价⽐。
据 The Information 近⽇报道，通过 OpenRouter 调⽤的 AI 模型中，使⽤量排名前⼗的⾼性能模型
占⽐已从 2025 年第⼀季度的 55% 降⾄ 2026 年第三季度的 14% 。随着低成本模型的能⼒不断提
升，越来越多⽤⼾发现，完成⽇常任务并不需要调⽤最昂贵的模型。
这意味着， AI ⾏业的竞争正在从单纯⽐拼模型性能，转向⽐拼性能、成本与实际应⽤价值。对于

Meta ⽽⾔，即使 Muse Wonder 能够在特定任务上达到领先⽔平，也仍需证明这种性能优势能够
转化为⽤⼾需求。
“ 前沿 ” 标签泛滥，模型性能竞赛仍在升温
近期，主要 AI 公司密集推出旗舰模型，并强调其技术领先性。⾕歌推出 Gemini 4 Argon ，称其在
多个领域的复杂⼯作流中达到前沿⽔平； OpenAI 发布 GPT-6 Astra ，宣称其在计算机操作、软件
⼯程、⽹络安全及专业⼯作等领域达到最先进⽔平； Anthropic 则推出 Claude Fable 5.1 和
Mythos 5.1 ，将其定位为全球领先的编程与知识⼯作模型。
Meta 推出 Muse Wonder ，也是在这⼀背景下重新强调⾃⾝的技术竞争⼒。经历约 14 个⽉的⼤规
模招聘扩张后，公司希望借助新模型证明⾃⼰仍处于 AI 前沿。不过，当各家⼚商都在强调性能
领先时， “ 前沿 ” 本⾝越来越难以成为差异化优势。
据报道， Meta 计划将 Muse Wonder ⽤于⽀持其个⼈智能体产品 Muse 。但新模型的技术表现能否

转化为⽤⼾可感知的体验提升，以及这种提升是否⾜以⽀撑更⾼的使⽤成本，仍有待验证。
低成本模型崛起，⽤⼾更看重性价⽐
OpenRouter 的数据揭⽰了需求端的变化：⾼性能模型的使⽤占⽐⼤幅下降，反映出⽤⼾正在重
新权衡模型能⼒与调⽤成本。随着低成本模型不断进步，许多⽇常任务已不再需要最昂贵的模
型。
企业尤其倾向于根据任务难度分配模型：常规⼯作交给价格更低的模型，只有复杂推理、专业
分析等⾼要求任务才调⽤顶级模型。这种做法既能满⾜实际需求，也能控制 AI ⽀出。
消费者市场也⾯临类似变化。对普通⽤⼾⽽⾔，模型能否准确回答问题、顺利完成任务，往往
⽐其是否达到⾏业性能榜单的顶端更重要。当 “ ⾜够好⽤ ” 的模型能够以更低价格满⾜⼤部分需求
时，顶级模型即使持续刷新性能纪录，也未必能获得相应的使⽤增⻓。
对 Meta 等科技巨头⽽⾔，挑战已不只是推出更强的模型，还在于证明性能提升能够带来真实的
⽤⼾需求。在 AI 竞争从追逐性能转向衡量性价⽐的过程中，技术领先与商业成功之间的关系正在
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 214: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1).

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort…

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).

With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION / EUROZONE
Resilient corporate sentiment, upside inflation surprises. The Euro -
pean Commission’s Economic Sentiment Index for September showed
continued recovery in industry (+1.2 pts to -3.8, a 3.5 year high), with
a clear rebound in production and employment expectations, while

services improved further (+0.5 pt to 6.1). By contrast, household
confidence and sentiment in construction weakened further, as did
the composite index. These surveys broadly corroborate the PMIs,
with the September manufacturing PMI revised up by 0.2 pt to 52.9.
The unemployment rate held steady at a low 6.4%. Meanwhile, the
September flash inflation estimate came in above consensus at 3.8%.
The increase was driven mainly by energy (18.8% y/y, +4.5 pp) and

services (3.2%, +0.2 pp). Non-energy industrial goods inflation eased
slightly to 1.1% y/y, but the trend is firming: the 3m/3m annualised
rate is at a three-year high of 2.5%. Speaking before the data release,
ECB President Lagarde argued for a “measured response” and noted
that the increase in bond yields was delivering some of the needed
restraint. Tightening is also occurring via the cost-of-borrowing for
mortgage loans, which increased in August for the fifth consecutive

month to 3.6% (+6 bp m/m), a level unseen since September 2024.
It was unchanged for loans to non-financial corporations (3.8%).
Upcoming: ECB minutes (Thursday) and EU Trade Commissioner
Šefcovic's visit to Beijing (Thursday-Friday)
France. In the eye of the bond market storm. The government’s draft
budget for 2027, despite targeting significant adjustment (see our
detailed analysis), failed to stop the surge in the OAT yield, which
passed 4.9% on Friday morning (a high unseen since 2002), while the

OAT-bund spread reached a peak of 151 bps before easing somewhat
along with the global bond market selloff. (The peak coincided with
a statement by Bundesbank President Nagel that the ECB’s instru -
ments are focused on monetary policy transmission, not any spread
level). As expected, the draft budget law targets a deficit of 5% of GDP
(down from 5.4% in 2026), implying EUR 54 bn of savings, of which
about 2/3 coming from reduced spending. France’s independent fiscal

watchdog commended the spending moderation effort but pointed out
optimistic economic assumptions.
Meanwhile, inflation surprised on the upside: HICP inflation accele -
rated to 3.4% y/y in September (consensus: 3.1%), from 2.6% in August.
The sharp rise in energy prices (21.2%) was amplified by a 10% y/y
increase in the price of fresh food (linked to the summer drought).
Core inflation rose to 1.6% y/y in September, up from 1.3% in August
(services inflation at 2.2%; +0.3 pp m/m). The rise in industrial pro -

ducer prices accelerated to 6% y/y in August from 4.3% in July (+67%
versus +50% for refined products). Higher fuel prices also weighed on
household consumption, which fell by -0.5% m/m in August. On the
brighter side, the September manufacturing PMI was revised up by
0.3 points to 50.6.
Germany. Inflation accelerates to 3-year high. HICP inflation reached
3.3% y/y in September (vs. 2.9% in August and 3.2% expected) ac -
cording to the flash estimate (the highest since December 2023),

driven by energy prices. Core inflation rose by 2.4% (same as in Au -
gust) and services inflation edged down to 2.7% (vs. 2.8% in August).
The unemployment rate was stable in September at 6.4%.
Upcoming: August factory orders (Tuesday) and industrial production
(Wednesday)
Italy and Spain. Upside inflation surprises, business sentiment gains.
In Italy, HICP rose by 4.1% (vs. 3.2% in August), while core rose by 1.7%
(vs. 1.5% in August). In Spain, HICP inflation accelerated to 5% y/y

(vs. 4.6% in August), while core inflation reached 3.1% y/y (vs. 2.9%
earlier). Business sentiment, measured by the European Commission’s
ESI, rose slightly in both manufacturing and services in Italy, and
more significantly in Spain, where unlike in the rest of Europe, consu -
mer sentiment improved as well. Less positively, Italy’s unemploy -
ment rate unexpectedly rose to 6.2%.
UNITED KINGDOM
Bold new policy paths and upward growth revision. Prime Minister

Burnham broke several policy taboos in his Labour Party Conference
Speech, notably reconsidering Brexit and abandoning the so-called
“triple lock” guaranteeing that state pensions rise each year by the
highest of inflation, wages, or 2.5%. These proposals would be taken
up in the next Parliament. He also vowed to dismantle the legacy
of Thatcherism in public services and utilities. Real GDP growth for
Q2 2026 was revised upward to 0.5% q/q (vs. 0.4% previously, fol -

lowing 0.6% in Q1), driven by a stronger-than-expected performance
in trade and investment. Inflation expectations in the Bank of En -
gland’s Decision Maker Panel rose to 3.9% for own prices and 3.3%
for CPI one year ahead.
UNITED STATES
Solid growth, underwhelming payrolls. Q2 GDP growth was revised
up to 2.2% annualised (from 1.5%), with broad-based revisions to in -
vestment and consumption. The September ISM manufacturing in -
dex edged down (-0.1 pt to 54.5) on a drop in production. However,

new orders, a leading indicator, improved, and the employment in -
dex held at its highest level in four years. Payrolls rose by 29,000
in September, well below expectations, while the two months prior
were revised down by a combined 60,000. Job gains in healthcare
(+23k) and leisure/hospitality (+10k) were partly offset by losses in
Government (-10k). The unemployment rate rose to 4.2% (+0.1pp)
on higher labour force participation. Private hourly earnings growth

continued to slow (3% y/y, lowest since mid-2021). The August per -
sonal consumption expenditure (PCE) inflation report surprised to
the downside (3.4% vs. 3.7% expected), and core PCE was unchanged
at 3%. On the household side, the saving rate fell sharply (4.1% of
disposable income in August, from 4.6% in July), explaining the unex -
pected resilience of consumption (+0.6% in August inflation-adjusted).
All in all, this suggests the Fed will continue to raise interest rates

but doesn’t need to rush. We expect the next 25 bps hike in December.
Upcoming: September ISM services (Monday); FOMC Minutes (We -
dnesday).
JAPAN
Booming business sentiment. The Tankan survey index of large ma -
nufacturers rose to +24 (+2 pts q/q), while small and medium en -
terprises saw significant gains (+14; +5 pts q/q and +23; +5 pts q/q
respectively), driven by demand for semiconductors and production
equipment. Services sentiment weakened slightly (+35; -2 pts q/q for

large firms). Investment intentions remain strong among large cor -
porations and are showing signs of recovery in SMEs.

EMERGING ECONOMIES
Manufacturing PMIs generally stronger. The index rose to 56.7 in
Taiwan (the highest level since 2021) and 53.9 in South Korea, boosted
by strong global demand for AI goods. Korean exports increased by
a record 83.5% y/y in September (of which chips: +263% y/y), mostly
led by export prices. The manufacturing PMI also improved and was

comfortably above 50 in India, Indonesia and Thailand. It declined
slightly in Vietnam (to 51.9). Malaysia and the Philippines stood out
with PMIs falling slightly below 50. PMIs also generally improved
in central Europe. In Latin America, PMIs were more mixed: falling
sharply in Brazil to 44.8, its lowest level since early 2023, but slightly
improving in Mexico (to 50) and Colombia (to 54.6).
ASIA
China. Better outlook on small stimulus and higher PMIs. Following

a string of data showing too sluggish domestic demand to meet the
Government’s growth target of 4.5 to 5%, the State Council announced
a package of stimulus measures, including the expansion of some re -
lending facilities aimed at financing tech/innovation, agriculture and
SMEs; new spending to be financed by carried-over local govern -
ment debt quotas; and a small interest rate subsidy on mortgages.
The latest PMIs published by the NBS and by RatingDog improved

marginally, with both now showing expansion in both manufacturing
and services.
EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST
Poland. Strong uptick in September’s inflation reading. Inflation
came in at 4.0% y/y in September (the highest level since June
2025) after 3.4% y/y in August, driven by higher prices at the pump.
The increase led the government to lower its VAT rate on fuel to 8%
(23% previously) from October until year-end, which should help ease
inflationary pressures. Overall, inflation may not significantly exceed

the Central bank’s target on average in 2026. The Central Bank may
nonetheless consider one rate hike by year end.
Türkiye. A financial scandal with limited macro impact. A Ponzi sche-
me collapse has impacted around 455k retail investors with assets
reportedly worth about 1% of GDP . The crisis was swiftly handled
by the authorities to prevent financial contagion. 131 funds and two
home-finance institutions involved in the scandal were suspended

and taken over by public banks for orderly and fair disposal of their
assets. Three investment banks and 2 factoring companies were
transferred to the Savings Deposit Insurance Fund. So far, 217 people
have been subjected to legal action, of whom 90 have been jailed
pending trial. Since September 9, the BIST-30 index lost 10% but is
still 25% higher than at end-2025, and foreign investors bought equi -
ties at the highest pace in 3 months last week, suggesting a buy-the-

dip reading of the situation. There has been no or marginal spillover
effects on the exchange rate, domestic interest rates and sovereign
spreads. At this stage, contagion risk to the banking sector is very low
but the damage to small investors may have negative spillovers on
consumption through negative wealth effect. Separately, the manu -
facturing PMI fell further in September to 47.9, signaling deepening
contraction.
Saudi Arabia. Budget deficit jumps on cost of war. Budget deficit is

now forecasted at USD 65.4 bn this year (-4.9% of GDP), up from
USD 44.3 bn (-3.3% of GDP) in the initial financing bill. Revenue is
expected to be slightly higher (+3.7%) as high oil prices more than
offset the decline in oil export volume. But spending has been revised
up by 9.3%. The new growth projection is also bearish, with GDP
expected to contract by -3.6% due to the decline in oil activity while
consensus sees economy growing by +0.2%.
LATIN AMERICA

Brazil. A tight runoff, a tough policy mix inheritance. Senator Flávio
Bolsonaro and Brazil’s incumbent President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva
will face off in a runoff on October 25 th after neither won a majority
in Sunday’s general election (47% vs. 45% of the vote respectively).
Since Brazil returned to democracy in 1989, no candidate who trailed
in the first round has won the runoff. The next President will inherit
a slowing economy and a difficult policy mix: pressing ahead with

fiscal consolidation while normalising monetary policy amid recur -
ring supply shocks (adverse weather conditions, El Niño, war in the
Middle East).
ENERGY
Energy. Limited relief, electricity excepted. Re-escalation risks ap -
pear to be rising in the very short term in the Gulf and the Red Sea.
At the same time, the record high level of Gulf oil exports in Sep -
tember (above 80% of pre-war level including bypass and trade shutt-
le) has been enough to stabilize prices at a high level, albeit not to

put significant and lasting downward pressure on crude prices.
- Crude oil: prices declined slightly over the week but remain high
(-3.5% w/w to USD 101.6/b on Monday morning for Brent). The gap
with physical barrel prices (Dated Brent) is rising again USD 24.4/b on
Friday), signalling persisting tensions on the physical market.
- The diesel market got some relief from a G7 decision late on Friday
to release 100 mb of oil and diesel emergency stocks, focused on the

latter. It should take place over the next four months and reportedly
puts off the table US export restrictions. The European benchmark
fell markedly (-12% w/w to USD80/b on Monday morning). However,
market pressure could rise again soon as most Chinese refiners an -
nounced the suspension of fuel exports for October as the government
want to prioritize domestic supply security.
- EU wholesale electricity prices rose sharply in September, reaching

EUR 146/MWh on average, the highest level since Dec 2022.

Executive summary

台灣半導體暨科技產業公布 9 ⽉份及 3Q26 營收，我們以公布的數據看產業趨勢
變化及公司與彭博社分析師預期進⾏分析⽐較，投資⼈須審慎評估。總⽽⾔
之， 3Q26 營收明顯超過彭博社分析師預期的公司⼤幅多於低於預期的公司達 6
倍多，⽽超過 53% 年增（⽤晶圓代⼯的 9 ⽉份營收年增做標竿）的有 6 個次產
業。除此以外，我們還提供了 3Q26 營收年增明顯改善及惡化的前 20 名公司整
理及排序，及 3Q26 營收明顯季增 / 季減的前 20 名公司整理及排序。總結⽽
⾔，伺服器產業鏈（對標公司有 Nvidia, AMD, Intel, ⾕歌 TPU ， Dell,
SuperMicro) ，存儲記憶體（對標公司有三星 , SK Hynix, SanDisk,
Western Digital) ， CCL/PCB ，半導體建廠及測試產業 3Q26 營收（對標公司
有  Teradyne, Advantest, Form Factor ）明顯⾼於分析師預期， 3Q26 營收
年增改善明顯的有存儲記憶體，光纖通訊（對標公司有 Lumentum,

Fabrinet, Coherent ）， CoWoS/CoPoS 先進封測設備產業。 3Q26 營收季增
明顯改善的產業有 CoWoS/CoPoS 先進封測設備產業，設計服務（對標公司有
Broadcom, Marvell) ，存儲記憶體，光纖通訊，及伺服器產業鏈。

9 ⽉  yoy > 8 ⽉  yoy 10 個百分點以上： 9 ⽉份營收年增率⽐ 8 ⽉明顯加

及 PC 產業鏈組裝下游如廣達及緯創，滑軌⿓頭川湖，電源管理晶⽚茂達

## Document 263: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova
Date:    8 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL:
French Toast & Brazilian Bossa
Nova
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 10 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Insaanu Studio / Unsplash

I. Bonds
The global bond market remains jittery, though yields may be starting to
stabilize after their rout in recent months (chart). Nevertheless, geopolitical
turbulence may still buffet them.

### Page 2

Yields have risen for an assortment of reasons, from better-than-expected
economic growth (US) to fiscal instability concerns (France). Brazil and
China are the only major countries showing declines in bond yields so far
this year (chart).
Bond yields have also risen on higher-for-longer oil prices as the war in
the Middle East has continued to disrupt oil supplies. The Houthis have
said airliners should avoid Saudi airspace, declaring it a zone of military

operations after the Saudi-backed Yemeni government launched an
offensive  against the group earlier this month. On Saturday, a third attack
this week on Riyadh's international airport left 12 people dead and dozens
wounded. President Donald Trump said the US will "look at" joining Saudi
strikes on Houthis.

### Page 3

II. Stay Home vs Go Global
So far this year, the US has held onto its 65% market-cap share of the All
Country World (ACW) MSCI (chart). Japan's share has also held steady at
5%. However, Europe's share has slipped (13.3%) to the advantage of the
share of the Emerging Markets (12.0%).
Nevertheless, last week, the US outperformed the Developed World ex-
US as the French debt crisis weighed on European stock prices (chart).
So far this month, the US MSCI is the second-best-performing stock price

index we track (chart). Brazil was the best-performing in response to first-
round presidential election results showing conservative Flavio Bolsonaro,
son of former President  Jair Bolsonaro, exceeding expectations in the
race against leftist President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva.

### Page 4

III. Earnings & Valuation
The ratio of forward earnings for the US MSCI to the ACW ex US MSCI
fell through the summer this year (chart). It stopped doing so in recent
weeks.
Forward P/Es have been falling worldwide since the start of the year, as
forward earnings have outpaced stock price gains (chart).

### Page 5

IV. Currencies
The French debt crisis is also weighing on the euro, which is down from
$1.20 at the start of this year to $1.12 (chart).
As a result, the DXY dollar index has rebounded to its best reading since
early 2025 (chart). We remain constructive on the US dollar.

### Page 6

V. Notable
(1) German factory orders fell sharply in August, plunging 10.6% m/m
(chart). The primary driver behind the contraction was a steep pullback in
large-scale contracts. The headline drop was almost entirely due to a
61.5% slump in large-scale orders for aircraft, ships, trains, and military
vehicles, which suffered a major correction following an exceptional surge
in July. When these volatile large-scale contracts are excluded, new
manufacturing orders actually slipped by just 0.1%, indicating that core

industrial demand was essentially treading water rather than experiencing
a broad-based collapse.
(2) CQQQ continues to underperform QQQ (chart). While China's long-
term industrial policy goals are ambitious, cyclical demand inside China

### Page 7

has struggled to generate the same explosive revenue growth seen in
Western tech sectors. Furthermore, China's tech industries carry a
persistent regulatory discount. Chinese technology sectors have
historically been vulnerable to abrupt policy shifts, anti-monopoly
crackdowns, data security reviews, and ongoing geopolitical tensions
(including US–China trade restrictions and semiconductor export
controls), which compress valuations and deter long-term institutional

clause.

### Page 8

Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, held at –0.03% in September,
unchanged from August.

The Leading Index continues to show moderately improved
momentum compared to the middle of the year, with the
latest reads pointing to growth holding at or slightly below
trend heading into late 2026/early 2027. Growth appears
to be stabilising at a slow pace rather than stalling. That
said, both the latest fuel prices and the RBA’s September
interest rate increase are still working their way through to
the economy with impacts likely to take some of the gloss off

growth near term. Overall, Westpac expects annual growth
to come in at 1.5% for calendar 2026, a below-trend result.
The Leading Index growth rate has firmed slightly over the
last six months, rising from –0.16% back in March to –0.03%
in September. The component detail shows mixed moves over
the period. Four components have contributed more positively
with reduced drags from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Unemployment Expectations Index (adding +0.23ppts to

the Index growth rate); the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Expectations Index (adding another +0.15ppts);
and the S&P/ASX200 (+0.15ppts) combining with a modest
lift in growth in US industrial production (adding +0.12ppts).
These positives have been partially offset by modest drags
from four previously stable components: dwelling approvals
(–0.34ppts); monthly hours worked (–0.08ppts); commodity
prices, measured in AUD terms (–0.07ppts) and the yield

spread (–0.05ppts).
Overall, the component mix speaks to a lack of direction.
Indeed, despite some large shocks over the last year, the
Leading Index growth rate has been relatively contained since
early 2025, holding in a narrow –0.3% to +0.5% range. This
compares to history which, outside of the COVID and GFC
shocks, has seen growth rates move between a much wider
–1.9% to +1.6% range. Of course, the apparent stability,
which is evident across all components of the index, may not

last but for now it suggests the economy has been stuck in a
bit of a holding pattern over the last two years.
Leading Index still tracking a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

the monthly Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index. We will
continue to publish regular bulletin updates through our westpaciq.
com.au website.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
NOVEMBER CPI
PREVIEW AND ANALYSIS
Complete Montly CPI will take some time to understand
19 December 2025
Key points
• The October monthly CPI was 
flat in the month, broadly as we 
expected but stronger than market 
expectations. For more information see 
“Electrical noise is far from static”.
• The impact on our inflation profile 
was marginal. The December quarter 
CPI remains 0.6%qtr/3.6%yr, while 
the Trimmed Mean edged up from 
0.7%qtr/3.1%yr to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr.
• Our November monthly CPI estimate 
is 0.4% for the month, leaving the 
annual pace unchanged at 3.8%yr.
• The introduction of the complete 
monthly CPI is a significant enhancement 
to our analysis of price formation in 
the Australian economy. However, 
with many series having only a short 
history, it will take time to fully 
understand some of the finer details.
• For now, we will continue to place 
greater emphasis on the quarterly 
measures of core inflation.

### Page 2

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
No surprise in the October CPI
The new complete monthly CPI gained 3.8% in the year to 
October, coming in a touch softer than Westpac’s estimate of 
3.9%yr but a bit stronger than the market estimate of 3.6%yr. 
So while the market and other commentators may have been 
surprised by the result it was broadly within our expectations 
and as such has had just a marginal impact on our inflation 
profile.
The October CPI was flat in the month, a touch softer than 
Westpac’s published near-cast of 0.1% on the back of a larger 
than expected decline in electricity (–10.2%) which was only 
partially offset by a smaller than expected decline in auto fuel 
(–0.9%) and an unexpected bump up in garments (+2.6%).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.3% in the month of October, 
the same monthly increase it has seen for the previous two 
months and down from the 0.5%mth increase in July but 
stronger than the 0.2%mth prints from March to June.
This saw the annual pace of the TM increase to 3.3% from 
3.2% in September. This month the TM excluded the annual 
rise in electricity; alongside other large price rises and falls. 
Due to the short history of many series in the monthly CPI, the 
annual pace of monthly TM inflation can only be calculated 
back to April 2025. Before then the ABS notes that the annual 
pace is calculated from the quarterly CPI data.
For more information on the October monthly CPI see 
“Electrical noise is far from static ”.
Monthly CPI points to some momentum
Following the October CPI we have seen some minor revisions 
to the various components in our December quarter CPI 
estimate with the upward revisions, such as transport via auto 
fuel and motor vehicles, being offset by downward revisions 
to recreation, alcohol & tobacco and utilities in particular. This 
has left our December CPI estimate unchanged at 0.6%qtr.   
As our Chief Economist, Luci Ellis, has noted that inflation 
did see a bump in the September quarter and again in the 
month of October. However, the main sources of the surprise 
have had little to do with domestic demand or labour market 
pressures. Rather, a sizeable part of the bump looks to have 
been from administered prices and noise. Since the release of 
the data, the RBA has recognised this issue but at the post 
RBA Board Decision Media Conference in December, the 
Governor communicated that the Bank is more worried about 
upside risks to inflation than first thought.
We agree that some of this momentum will continue into the 
December quarter as highlighted by our upward revision in our 
We will continue to focus on the quarterly prints
Justin Smirk
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
December quarter TM estimate to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr from our 
earlier estimate of 0.7%qtr/3.1%yr. 
For the December quarter, price changes that are included in 
the TM go from a low of –0.26% to a high of 1.66%. Readers 
will remember that the TM is calculated from seasonally 
adjusted price changes and not the original estimates so if 
unaffected by regular seasonal price shifts. We estimate 
that the most significant components to be trimmed off 
the top in the quarter are: electricity (2.7%); other services 
for motor vehicles (2.5%); tobacco (2.1%); and automotive 
fuel (1.7%). Trimmed off the bottom are: other non-durable 
household products (–0.39%); garments for women (–0.92%); 
international holiday travel (–0.97%); vegetables (–1.16%) and 
wine (–1.27%).   
However, we do not expect this momentum to continue 
through 2026. As Luci noted in her article “ RBA on hold 
in 2026, risks on both sides ” we expect inflation to keep 
easing, due to the lagged impact of past restrictive policy 
and the unwind of earlier government support measures. We 
believe that many commentators have overlooked these lags. 
Monetary policy typically takes a year or more to fully affect 
jobs and prices, so current outcomes reflect the peak cash rate 
from a year ago and we expect to see further labour market 
softening. Strong hiring across the care economy has masked 
some of that restrictiveness, as has public infrastructure 
spending, but these supports are now fading along with last 
year’s tax-cut boost. We believe that even with the 75bps in  
rate cuts this year, policy remains mildly restrictive, and given 
usual lags, this will keep showing in the data through 2026.
Taking a look at some of the recent larger moves in monthly 
prices can highlight many of the issues Luci raises. Taking the 
seasonally adjusted data, smoothing it with a three month 
average then calculating the change over three months (so we 
have three months on three months percentage change) we 
find at the top end of the list some price increases you would 
not expect to continue at the same pace. 
For example at the top are: lamb & goat (8.1%, highly 
seasonal); accessories (8.1% with jewellery prices surging 
on the back of record high gold prices); audio visual & 
computing services (6.0% due to Netflix subscriptions); beef 
& veal (3.7%, highly seasonal); coffee & tea (3.6% noting 
global coffee prices have passed their peak and are already 
significantly lower); water & sewerage (2.3% IPART decision 
was for a 13.8% increase for Sydney Water in October); 
property rates & charges (2.3% administered prices); and 
electricity (1.2%, reflecting unwinding of cost of living 
rebates). Offsetting some of these increases was falling prices 
for: men’s footwear (–2.0%); vegetables (–2.0% seasonal); 
eggs (–1.5%, reflecting the unwinding of the price spike 
associated with an outbreak of bird flu); and major household 
appliances (–1.2%). 
Core inflation still to moderate through 2026
We are projecting headline inflation to get back to the RBA’s 
target band, but not until later in 2026. We now see core 
inflation back below the midpoint of the 2–3% range at the 
end of the year. We had thought this might be achieved by the 
third quarter but now expect current momentum to continue

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
for a bit longer. However, it is worth noting that our estimate
of the six month annualised pace of the TM is down to 2.4%yr
by June 2026.
Electricity to boost November CPI
Westpac is estimating a 0.4% increase in the November

monthly CPI which will hold the annual pace flat at 3.8%yr.
The seasonally adjusted estimate is 0.5% in the month
suggesting it is a stronger than usual estimate.
Boosting the November estimate is: a 16% surge in electricity
(as the impact of the last round of rebates fades); a 3.2% jump
in gas & other household fuels; and a 2.3% bump in auto fuel
(based on reported pump prices). Partially offsetting these
gains is a –2.6% decline in holiday travel & accommodation

and a –2.9% drop in clothing & footwear.
Take care with the Monthly CPI
Many series in the monthly CPI only date back to early 2024
so seasonal adjustment is somewhat problematic. Some
series did have a longer monthly history coming from the
previously published monthly CPI indicator and it appears
the ABS may have used some of this to seasonally adjust the
data. However, we caution that some of the new data has a
different history to the old data and as such, we expect it is

going to take some time to understand the seasonal behaviour
of the new data.
For example, for male garments there is some difference
with the reported monthly change compared to the old CPI
Indicator series but this appears to be only marginal. However,
for women’s, the difference can be significant with even a
reversal of the sign of change in some recent months. And for
some series with a short history and no monthly indicator data
to compare to some of the adjustment appears to be a little

arbitrary, as you can see in the chart right which compares the
children’s garments index in original and seasonally adjusted
terms. The only adjustment made to the series was in June
2025 when a –3.2% change was adjusted to a flat print. Why?
We are yet to uncover.
You can also see where the ABS has turned to smoothing
processes to seasonally adjust short data series. In the chart
over the seasonally adjusted series looks as if has been
generated by applying a Henderson Trend to the original

children’s footwear series.
As such, we expect it is possible there will be significant
adjustments made to the seasonally adjustment process as
data is collected which could result in significant revisions to
seasonally adjusted series including the Trimmed Mean and
the Weighted Median.

◼ 貨幣政策與殖利率： 聯準會言論放鷹是為重塑
公信力，9月按兵不動仍為較高機率情境，然不
能排除聯準會為了強化政策訊號而進行 「象徵
性升息 」的可能性 。推演美債長天期殖利率
2H26偏高表現、1H27適度回落。
◼ 投資建議： 企業獲利和景氣維持成長 ，利率上
揚壓抑股市估值但研判為暫時性 ，高殖利率信
用債在緩升息環境下表現仍穩健 ，惟債務風險
未緩解，利率敏感度高的長債價格波動較大。
熱門議題/資產影響推估 市場焦點
➢ 8月以來全球公債殖利率上升：美國10年期公債利
率上探4.8%，30年期公債殖利率創下 2007年6月
以來新高，日本10年期公債殖利率創下 1996年以
來新高。主要受財政壓力 、地緣不確定性 、貨幣
政策等多重因素帶動。
➢ 推演殖利率 2H26偏高表現、1H27適度回落：本
行預估聯準會 2026年尚不至採取升息 ，然鷹派言
論將讓高利率環境短期難解 ，有待2027年通膨壓

力緩解，利率方能適度回落。

美國10年期公債殖利率站上4.8%，股市情緒受到影響但未失控
美債殖利率曲線短端因反應通膨擔憂而上升
盤勢回顧
股市情緒受到影響但未失控，信用債普遍收紅
3
• 升息擔憂推升 10年期公債殖利率站上 4.8%，但信用債過去一個月總報酬仍為正值 ：聯準會主席華許在8月底
Jackson Hole央行年會釋出鷹派訊號，市場提高對9月升息的押注，再加上近期中東地緣政治風險又再推動油價
上漲，美國10年期公債殖利率站上4.8%整數關卡，短天期利率主要反映聯準會升息預期升幅高於長天期利率。利
率走升雖對信用債帶來壓力，但利差收斂及息收仍為總報酬帶來貢獻，過去一個月美國非投資級債及新興主權
債仍上漲0.5%，就連長天期公司債亦仍收紅。
• 股市情緒受到影響但未失控：華許於7月底FOMC會議表示通膨有進展，然8月偏鷹派表述之後股市情緒受到一
定程度影響，不過修正幅度不大，並未出現恐慌情緒。而長天期利率其實在 8月初就開始出現上行趨勢 ，不過
多數指數8月以來仍收正報酬，僅利率敏感性較強的地產和公用事業表現較為落後。

殖利率％
殖利率變化（基點）

### Page 4

©  CTBC
「財政壓力+地緣不確定性+AI支出 」= 高利率環境短期難解
期限溢酬+升息預期共同推升殖利率居高不下
環境分析
資料來源：(左) NY Fed ，2026/9；(右)中國信託商業銀行編繪，2026/6。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/8
• 財政可持續性疑慮 +地緣衝突頻繁，推升風險溢價要求：觀察聯準會經濟學家的 ACM模型可發現 ，期限溢價
(承擔未來利率變動、通膨與供給風險所要求的額外補償報酬 )達80個基點，也是去年以來美債殖利率走勢和降
息趨勢背離的主因。其中，近年地緣政治風險不確定性提高，以及主要國家推動大規模財政政策，使得財政與
債務可持續性疑慮不斷升溫等結構性因素，是市場對持有長端債券要求的風險貼水不斷增加。
• AI資本支出進一步推升中性利率水準：短期間，AI浪潮帶動科技巨頭大幅增加資料中心、AI晶片、電力基礎設
施及雲端設備投資，使企業資本支出需求顯著上升，推升整體資金需求，帶動實質利率走揚。長期而言，隨著
AI帶動生產力的顯著提升，增強了企業投資報酬率與經濟體的潛在成長率，經濟便能承受較高利率水準，促使

市場上修中性利率(r*)預估。

### Page 5

©  CTBC
聯準會放鷹重塑公信力，核心目的仍為穩定長天期利率
升息預期降溫時期，長天期利率卻不降反升
事件說明
通膨已見高峰，然需待2Q27方見明顯下滑
• 華許放鷹，重燃升息預期：聯準會主席華許在7月底FOMC會議表示通膨有進展，8月公布的數據也驗證該看法，
升息預期一度大幅降溫，但8月底JacksonHole他卻展現相當鷹派的論調，9月升息的機率再度回升到50%以上。
• 鴿派論述無法壓制長天期利率，聯準會轉換策略：市場近期呈現一種較特殊的現象，當升息預期下降、短端利
率回落時，長天期公債殖利率卻不降反升，甚至迫使財政部透過公債回購政策來緩和壓力。這可能反映市場擔
憂聯準會過早轉向鴿派，進而削弱其長期抗通膨的公信力，促使部分投資人減持長天期美債。在此背景下，本
行認為華許轉趨鷹派的重要目的之一，是維持市場對聯準會抗通膨決心的信任。若9月公布的8月通膨數據符合預
期，9月FOMC按兵不動仍為較高機率情境。在聯準會維持鷹派但不升息的情境下，本行預估下半年美國10年期
公債殖利率的區間將處於4.5%-4.8%，美國30年期公債殖利率則在5.1%-5.5%之間。

資料來源：左， Bloomberg ，2026/9，右，高盛，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9

進入緊縮周期的機率低，短線影響股債行情但不影響趨勢
推演殖利率2H26偏高表現、1H27適度回落
貨幣政策
過往在景氣高峰下利率上揚，本益比多半承壓
• 進入緊縮周期的機率低：雖然不能排除聯準會為了強化政策訊號與維持公信力，而進行「象徵性升息」，也就是
短期的政策微調。但以目前的通膨與就業數據趨勢來看，並不會是新一輪升息循環的起點。通膨壓力在2027上
半年預估將有較明顯的緩解，屆時聯準會的立場將再轉換。
• 聯準會若升息，10年期利率或先上探5%，暫時壓抑股市估值：推演後續路徑，長天期殖利率將呈現2H26偏高
表現，1H27殖利率適度回落的格局。但若聯準會2H26升息，市場第一時間將因擔憂持續性升息的可能，而使
美國10年期公債殖利率先上探 5%。從長週期角度而言，利率與景氣及股市評價走勢亦步亦趨 ，不過當景氣處
於高峰時利率上揚，本益比多半承壓，因為景氣高峰意味企業獲利再成長空間有限，或股價已經被充分評價。
考量AI基礎建設資本支出週期尚未結束，企業獲利仍保持兩位數成長下，利率上揚對估值的壓抑研判為暫時性。

資料來源：左， 中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9，右，Bloomberg，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9

### Page 7

©  CTBC
日本債匯承壓，利差交易平倉與干預日圓皆對美債形成干擾風險
外部風險
資料來源：(左)Bloomberg，2026/8；(右)日本央行，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9
• 《日本成長戰略》+史上最大預算案，日債匯市面臨拋售壓力：5月日本政府擬推動規模達370兆日圓的《日本
成長戰略》後，市場對財政可持續性的疑慮快速升溫，5~8月內外資合計賣超20兆日圓日本公債；隨後日本政
府提出2027財年預算達到143兆日圓、年增16%，加上美國持續對日本升息施加壓力，日本利匯率持續承壓。
• 日本如暴力升息或引發利差交易平倉衝擊美債，然債務壓力限制下機率不高：今年美日利差在日本貨幣政策正
常化後罕見擴大，利差交易餘額(日本金融機構轉移海外資產 )較過去兩年明顯增加，若日本升息過快或日圓快
速升值，觸發利差交易平倉至美債部位減碼影響較大。同時，若日圓貶值壓力失控，財務省加大出售美元資產
進行匯市干預，可能增加美債賣壓推升殖利率，加劇金融市場波動。然美國已設立FIMA Repo機制避免日本大

量賣出美債；而日本債務壓力承重、升息速度受限，加以美債利率維持高檔，短期利差交易大量平倉誘因有限。

投資觀點
資料來源：(左) Bloomberg、BofA，2026/4/7；(右) Bloomberg，2026/9/1 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/1

挫，然而貨幣政策不變仍為目前基本情境，再者，每次升息大環境不同，美國通膨已見觸頂向下、也不像2022

升，經濟成長持穩支撐信用債上漲。預期在企業獲利及景氣成長循環未結束前，非投資級債這類高殖利率資產
在穩健債信支持下波動可望降低，惟債務風險未緩解，利率敏感度高的長債(尤其是25年以上)價格波動仍大。
• 短線利率反彈，雖然基本面支持輪動，但資金高度參與下須保留部位彈性：過去利率自波段低點回升期間，股
市多能同步上漲，反映殖利率上升通常與景氣及企業獲利改善有關。本次多了財政壓力的結構性因素，限縮未

來幾個月的指數空間，但企業獲利穩健成長的基本趨勢不變，資金並未撤出股市，而是轉向加快輪動布局速度，
投資上仍建議降低單一主題集中度，並且適度提高部位彈性，利用市場震盪時布局投資機會。

## Document 440: ハンガリーは凍結された復興基金を受け取れるか？ ～「脱オルバン」改革、ＥＵが最終審査～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ハンガリーは凍結された復興基金を受け取れるか？  ～「脱オルバン」改革、ＥＵが最終審査～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年 10 月２日

1/2 
ハンガリーは凍結された復興基金を受け取れるか？  
～「脱オルバン」改革、ＥＵが最終審査～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ ハンガリーは９月30 日、 欧州復興基金の最終支払い請求を欧州委員会に提出した。 オルバン
前政権下では、司法の独立性や汚職、公共調達の透明性などを巡る懸念から資金提供が凍結
されてきたが、４月の政権交代後、マジャル政権はＥＵとの関係正常化に向けた改革を急速
に進めてきた。 新たな復興計画は総額約100 億ユーロで、 新政権は８月末までに必要な改革 ・
投資条件を達成したとしている。 ただ、 今回の申請によって資金受領が確定した訳ではなく、
欧州委員会が条件の達成状況を審査したうえで年末までに支払いの可否を判断する。巨額の
ＥＵ資金を受領できるかは、新政権による「脱オルバン」改革の実効性と、悪化したＥＵと
の関係修復の進展を占う試金石となる。

ハンガリーは９月30 日、欧州復興基金の最終支払い請求を欧州委員会に提出した。復興基金
は、コロナ危機からの経済復興に必要な財政資金を加盟国に提供する目的で2021 年に創設され、
南欧や東欧諸国に手厚く配分されてきた。復興基金を通じた財政支援は、①資金の一部を、返済
を前提とする融資ではなく、返済が不要な補助金として提供する点や、②気候変動対策、デジタ
ル化対応、ロシア産化石燃料依存の脱却といった欧州連合（ＥＵ）の優先課題、各国固有の構造
問題などに取り組むことを追加の資金受領の条件としている点が、従来の財政支援の枠組みと異
なる。復興基金からの追加の資金提供は2026 年末で終了する。追加の資金受領を希望する国は、
８月 31 日までに復興計画で合意した改革条件を達成し、それに基づいて、９月30 日までに支払
い請求をしなくてはならなかった。 
ハンガリーはオルバン前首相の治世下で、法の支配など欧州連合（ＥＵ）の基本価値、ロシア
制裁、ウクライナ支援などを巡って、ＥＵとの対立を繰り返してきた。司法の独立性、汚職問
題、公共調達の透明性などへの懸念があるとして、ＥＵはハンガリーに対する復興基金からの資
金提供を凍結してきた。資金提供を開始するには、司法の独立性やＥＵ予算の保護などに関する
27 項目の条件（これをスーパー・マイルストーンと呼ぶ）を達成する必要があった。状況が大き
く変わったのは、今年４月の総選挙で親ＥＵ政権が誕生したことだ（詳細は４月13 日付けレポー
ト「ハンガリーに親ＥＵ政権誕生へ」を参照されたい）。マジャル首相は５月にフォンデアライエ
ン欧州委員長と会談し、凍結されてきたＥＵ資金の解除に向けた改革に取り組むことを約束し

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
た。ハンガリー政府は前政権が提出した復興計画の見直しに着手し、６月に新たな計画を提出、
ＥＵはこれを７月に正式に承認した。 
新たな復興計画は、返済不要の補助金が約65 億ユーロ、返済が必要な融資が約 35 億ユーロの
総額約 100 億ユーロで、資金受領の条件として67 項目の改革と47 項目の投資が盛り込まれた。
新政権は発足直後から改革に着手し、公共放送の経営・編集体制の刷新、前政権が設置した治安
機関の廃止、汚職やＥＵ資金を監視する欧州検察庁への参加を決定したほか、７月には基本法
（憲法に相当）を改正し、①前政権が選出した大統領の早期退任、②憲法裁判所判事の70 歳定年
制の復活、③憲法裁判所による予算や税制関連法に対する審査権限強化、④最高裁判所や司法行
政トップの選出方法変更と任期短縮、⑤首相や国家議員の任期制限、⑥国家資産の回収・保護局
の創設、⑦議会の３分の２以上の賛成が必要な法律の範囲を縮小するなどの改革に着手した（詳
しくは８月12 日付けレポート「ハンガリーで急速に進む「脱オルバン」改革」を参照された
い） 。政府は８月末に、復興基金の受領に必要な条件を期限内に達成したと発表していた。 
今回の申請により、ハンガリーが復興基金からの資金を受領できると確定した訳ではない。欧
州委員会は提出された資料を基に、復興計画で記された定量・定性目標が達成されたかどうかを
判断する。条件を満たしていないと判断された場合、支払いが留保されたり、減額されたりする
可能性がある。条件未達と判断された部分について、来年以降に改革を追加して改めて資金を請
求することは基本的にできない。新たな復興計画は約100 億ユーロの総額のうち、事前資金とし
て受け取った部分を除く、約91 億ユーロが今回の最終審査の対象となる。欧州委員会は駆け込み
申請をした他の加盟国からの支払い請求とともに、11 月20 日頃までに暫定評価をまとめ、閣僚理
事会などの意見を踏まえ、12 月末までに支払い決定を行う予定だ。９月だけで21 ヶ国から総額
1,054 億ユーロの請求が提出されており、審査期間は極めて短い。 
 ハンガリーにとって今回の支払い請求は、単に約91 億ユーロのＥＵ資金を確保できるかを左右
するだけではない。オルバン前政権下で長年にわたりＥＵとの対立の火種となってきた法の支配
や汚職対策を巡り、４月に発足した新政権が僅か数ヶ月で進めた改革を、ＥＵがどこまで十分と
認めるかが本格的に試される。巨額のＥＵ資金の行方とともに、オルバン時代に悪化したＥＵと
の関係修復がどこまで進んだのかを占ううえでも、欧州委員会の審査結果が注目される。

## Document 446: ブラジル：右派政権発足の場合、何が変わるのか

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジル：右派政権発足の場合、何が変わるのか.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

新興国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス　〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全

2026 年 10 月 8 日　全 5 頁

ブラジル：右派政権発足の場合、何が変わる
のか 
市場志向を強めつつ、社会的包摂を維持するフラビオ氏、外交は実
利重視か

経済調査部 シニアエコノミスト 増川 智咲

[要約] 
 2026年10月4日に行われた大統領選挙の第1回投票では、ボルソナロ前大統領の長男
であるフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員（以下、フラビオ氏）が首位となったが、過半
数に届く候補者はいなかった。こうした接戦の背景には、現政権の社会福祉政策を評
価する声が根強い一方で、医療や治安、インフレ、低成長などの政策運営に対する不
満も投票行動を左右したとみられる。 
 ルラ政権下では、雇用や実質所得の上昇、信用拡大が消費を押し上げ、貧困削減と内
需主導の成長を支えた。ただし、その成長は資源価格の上昇に伴う交易条件の改善に
も支えられており、生産性向上や持続的な民間投資を通じた成長モデルへの転換は十
分には進まなかった。所得移転による再分配機能を維持しながら、このような構造問
題にも対応することが、次期政権に一段と求められている。 
 フラビオ氏が掲げる2027～30年の政府計画では、1990年代のような「小さな政府」は
追求されておらず、行政組織と歳出の選別、国有資産の民間開放、規制改革を進める
一方、政治的支持の高い社会政策と基礎的公共サービスを維持しようとする構想だ。 
 対外政策では、OECD 加盟やグローバルバリューチェーンへの参加、外資誘致を重視し
ており、少なくとも経済外交の面では、保護主義的な色彩を強める米国とは異なり、
国際経済への統合を重視する姿勢がうかがえる。またそれと並行して、貿易・投資上
の利益を踏まえてグローバルサウスとの関係も維持する、実利的な多角外交を選択す
る可能性が高い。 
 決選投票後の注目点は、右派か左派かの二者択一ではなく、社会的包摂と市場改革と
いった、これまで同国で相反するとされてきた政策をどのように両立していくのかだ
ろう。

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

フラビオ・ボルソナロ氏が首位、ルラ氏との決選投票へ 
2026年10月4日に行われたブラジル大統領選挙の第1回投票では、ボルソナロ前大統領の長
男であるフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員（以下、フラビオ氏）が有効票の 47.03％、ルラ現大
統領 1が 45.16％を獲得した。いずれも過半数に届かず、両氏は 10 月 25 日の決選投票に進むこ
ととなった。投票直前の主要世論調査も両氏の接戦を示していたが、実際の投票ではフラビオ
氏が多くの調査予測を上回った。こうした接戦の背景には、ルラ政権の社会福祉政策を評価す
る声が根強い一方で、医療や治安、インフレ、低成長などの政策運営に対する不満も投票行動
を左右したとみられる 2。

ルラ政権：社会政策が支えた内需拡大と残された課題 
ルラ第 1 次・第 2 次政権下では、2003 年に創設された、低所得者層に対する現金給付である
「ボルサ・ファミリア」や、2009 年に導入された「Minha Casa, Minha Vida（私の家、私の人
生） 」といった住宅供給政策を通して、低所得者層を対象とした社会政策を拡充してきた。筆
者が 2010年にブラジルで見学した低所得者向け住宅は、スラム街であるファベーラ住民向けに
供給されたものであった（図表 1） 。簡素な造りで、販売価格は必ずしも低廉ではなかったもの
の、衛生面や治安面ではファベーラを大きく上回り、需要は旺盛であった。また、ショッピン
グモールでは割賦販売が広く利用されており、信用拡大が消費を下支えする構造も見られた
（図表 2） 。 
ルラ政権下では、雇用や実質所得の上昇、信用拡大が消費を押し上げ、貧困削減と内需主導
の成長を支えた。図表 3 は所得階層別の世帯割合を示しているが、貧困層と低所得層とされる
DEクラスの割合が、ルラ第1次政権下の 2003年以降大きく低下し、中間層とされる Cクラスの
割合が大きく拡大したことが分かる。 
ただし、その成長は、資源価格の上昇に伴う交易条件の改善にも支えられており、生産性向
上や持続的な民間投資を通じた成長モデルへの転換は十分には進まなかった。2010 年代半ばに
資源ブームが終焉を迎えると、それまで良好な交易条件の下で覆い隠されてきた低い生産性や
競争力といった構造的な課題が顕在化した。図表 4 は、ブラジルと主要地域・所得国グループ
の労働生産性（労働者一人あたり GDP）を表したものであるが、中所得国全体と比較しても、
ブラジルの労働生産性の低さが目立つ。

1 2003 年から～2010 年まで第 1、2 期を務めた後、2023 年からは第 3 期を務めている。 
2 世論調査機関であるInstituto de Pesquisas Datafolha が 2026 年 5 月に発表した調査によると、現政権に
対する不満には、治安、医療、経済、汚職が挙げられていた。（Datafolha: áreas prioritárias para o 
eleitor são as piores avaliadas no governo Lula 2026 年 5 月 17 日）

### Page 3

1

3 / 5

図表 1 住宅供給政策で建設された住宅

（注） 「Minha Casa, Minha Vida」政策によって建設さ
れていた住宅。

な課題として認識されている。そのためには、規制・制度面の改善や対外開放、効率的な資源

がある。こうした投資の拡大が生産性の向上とより質の高い雇用の創出につながれば、所得の

安定を通じて、治安改善を含む社会の安定にも寄与するだろう。したがって、フラビオ政権が
発足した場合にも、政策課題は再分配か成長かの二者択一ではなく、社会的包摂と持続的な成
長をいかに両立させるかにある。

フラビオ氏は市場志向だが、社会的包摂も維持。実利外交の可能性が高い 
図表5は、フラビオ氏が掲げる2027～30年の政府計画である。これらを見ると、省庁の削減
や歳出削減・財政規律、民営化を通じて政府の効率性を高め、民間投資と外国直接投資を呼び
込む方針を示している。また、OECD 加盟、グローバルバリューチェーン 3への参加、競争力の
向上を重視しており、経済運営の軸足はルラ政権より市場志向になる可能性が高い。 
一方、同計画は既存の社会福祉プログラムの維持や、医療・教育面の強化にも言及している。
このため、フラビオ氏の路線を 1990年代の新自由主義的な「小さな政府」とみなすことは適切
ではない。むしろ、行政組織と歳出の選別、国有資産の民間開放、規制改革を進める一方、政
治的支持の厚い社会福祉政策と基礎的公共サービスを維持しようとする構想である。 
特に財政面では、省庁削減や行政改革、歳出の見直しを通じて財政規律を強化する一方、イ
ンフラや治安、医療、教育などの政策課題にも対応する方針を示している。ブラジルでは、政
府部門の貯蓄不足や歳出構造の硬直性が、高金利や民間投資を制約する一因となっており、財
政健全化と成長力強化を両立できるかが重要となる。 
対外政策では、フラビオ氏は親米派とされ、 BRICS を中心とするグローバルサウスとの距離
ができるとの見方もある。しかし、計画を見る限り、国際基準を満たすことによる OECD 加盟、
グローバルバリューチェーンへの参加、外資誘致を重視しており、少なくとも経済外交の面で
は、保護主義的な色彩を強める米国とは異なり、対外開放や国際経済への統合を重視する姿勢
がうかがえる。また、中国との緊密な貿易・投資関係に鑑みると、グローバルサウスと距離を
取ることによる経済的コストを甘受するとも考えにくい。対米関係の強化を図りつつ、貿易・
投資上の利益を踏まえてグローバルサウスとの関係も維持する実利的な多角外交を選択する可
能性が高いだろう。 
以上のように、右派と呼ばれるフラビオ政権が発足した場合も、 「小さな政府」ではなく、
社会福祉政策の維持と市場改革の政策パッケージが取られる可能性が高い。決選投票後の注目
点は、右派か左派かの二者択一ではなく、社会的包摂と市場改革といった、これまで同国で相
反するとされてきた政策をどのように両立していくのか、だろう。

3 製品やサービスが完成するまでの各工程を国際的に分業し、各国が付加価値を生み出しながら連結された生
産ネットワーク。

World Trends

1/3 
ロシア下院選、与党･統一ロシアが３分の２超の議席を維持 
～選挙結果は政権に追い風も、石油製品不足とインフレ再加速で国民生活には逆風が続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ロシアでは９月18～20 日に、 ウクライナ戦争開戦後初となる連邦議会下院 （国家院） 総選挙
が実施された。ロシア経済は、欧米の制裁下でも中国やインドなどとの経済関係や軍需関連
産業の拡大を追い風に持ち直してきたが、足元ではウクライナによる製油所やエネルギー・
輸送インフラへの攻撃が活発化し、石油製品の不足と価格上昇が国民生活を圧迫している。 
○ 総選挙では、反戦候補の多くが立候補を認められないなど政治活動への厳しい統制が続くな
か、 与党・統一ロシアが得票率57.97％を獲得し、355 議席を確保する見通しとなった。 投票
率も 59.32％と前回を上回り、同党は憲法改正に必要な３分の２以上の議席を維持した。他
の議席獲得政党も主要政策では政権と歩調を合わせることが多く、議会は事実上の「総与党
状態」にある。表面的には、選挙結果はロシア国内の結束を示すものとなった。 
○ 一方、 石油製品価格の上昇を背景にインフレ率は再加速しており、 ロシア中銀は９月に11 会
合ぶりに政策金利を据え置いた。今後は天候不順による食料価格上昇も懸念され、エネルギ
ーと食料という生活必需品を中心に国民生活への負担が一段と強まる可能性がある。総選挙
では政権与党の優位が確認されたが、その背後では経済・生活環境の厳しさが増している。

ロシアでは、９月18～20 日の日程で連
邦議会下院 （国家院： 定数450） 総選挙が
実施された。ロシア経済を巡っては、 ウク
ライナ戦争の開戦から４年半以上が経過
するなど 「戦時経済」 が長期化している。
開戦当初には、欧米などによる経済制裁
の強化を理由に、景気に大幅な下押し圧
力が掛かる事態に直面した。 しかし、 その
後は中国やインドなど多くの新興国が欧
米などの動きに同調せず、結果的に経済
制裁の 「抜け穴」 となる動きがみられた。
